# 0.19.0 — rulings

Recorded verbatim. A ruling's disposition says what it binds; a correction to a ruling is a new row,
never an edit of an old one. Rulings of 0.18.0 are cited as "0.18.0 D-n"
(`../../observer-maps/02-analysis/rulings.md`).

This log covers the paired train: watchpost 0.19.0, go-ionomaps (new; formerly go-giro-data, D-56) and go-tuiMaps v0.3.0. Each of
the other two keeps its own log for its own decisions; a decision that binds more than one is written
here and cited from theirs.

| # | Date | Question | HUM LEAD verbatim | Ruling |

PS-2 (the Observer ham): "An amateur HF operator using watchpost cannot tell, without leaving it, which bands will carry a signal between their place and the places they want to reach at a given hour, so they choose by habit or from a separate website, and call into bands that are closed or miss ones that are open." Metrics next, one statement at a time. |

| D-38 | 2026-10-06 | Is D-18's budget a host limit? (It is self-imposed; neither GIRO nor NOAA publishes a limit; GIRO has throttled once with a 429 and sends no rate-limit headers) | "Let's try to see if we can get our baseline data in single sitting (what watchpost would actually do) and see what happens.  If we get denied, then we can use this exercise to determine a realistic budget with throttle expectations built into the feature" | **Amends D-18 for wave 2:** the baseline data is fetched in one sitting, as watchpost itself would: sequential requests, no artificial spacing beyond the client's own pacing, every response's headers kept. On the first denial (429 or similar) the run stops and measures recovery with a bounded back-off. The outcome sets a realistic budget and the throttle behaviour built into the feature. KC2G stays excluded; Open-Meteo is never probed. |
| D-39 | 2026-10-06 | Replace D-18's courtesy budget with throttle behaviour built into the feature, sized from D-38's measurement (`wave2-findings.md`: GIRO a bucket of about 90 refilling at about 3 a minute, a bare 429 with no `Retry-After`, recovery within 60 s; NOAA no limit met) | "Adopt as recommended" | **Supersedes D-18's 5-a-day.** GIRO: an update asks only the live stations (about 38), the live list refreshed at most daily; one update's requests in one burst capped at 40, at most hourly; never more than 2 a minute sustained outside a burst. On a 429: stop the update, wait 60 s, then double up to 15 minutes; keep the last good field and say its age (FR-5); never retry faster. NOAA (if path D): the newest grid only, at most 6 an hour. These rules bind DISCOVER and every later phase. KC2G stays excluded; Open-Meteo is never probed. Every request's headers are logged, so a published limit or `Retry-After` is noticed the first time. |
| D-40 | 2026-10-06 | go-giro-data's path (D-20), from the leave-one-station-out scores on 2026-10-05 (`wave2-findings.md`; 566 pairs; foF2 RMS in MHz): climatology 1.38; D 1.14; B on climatology 1.12-1.17 (0.38 within 500 km); B on D 1.00 (0.37 within 500 km, best at every distance) | "B on D (Recommended)" | **The path is B on D:** go-giro-data assimilates GIRO station residuals over a background derived from NOAA GloTEC; a PyIRI-port climatology is the fallback when GloTEC is missing or stale (FR-5). Per copy, under D-39: about 38 GIRO requests and one NOAA grid an hour. PLAN's dry run (REFLECT L1) re-scores it over more days before targets are set. Both sources' terms apply: GIRO CC BY-NC-SA (whether the computed field is a "substantially derivative product" is OQ-G2, still to rule); GloTEC public domain. |

P10-07 enforces `errcheck` with `check-blank` off, so it checks `error` returns only and treats `_` as an intentional discard (li-A2DH `p10-checked-returns/SKILL.md`); a discarded `ok bool` is never examined. A syntactic local check (a module function returning `(T, bool)` whose `ok` is assigned to `_`) flags 28 sites: 9 in `platform/history` and `domains/`, 19 in `modes/` and `app/`. Options: the stores and sources now, the whole tree now, or the #27 fix alone and the lint later | First: "Local lint now, upstream later (Rec.)". | D-143 | 2026-10-08 | W1.2: FR-6.5 says each dataset has a byte bound beside its time retention. The Data tab offers up to 5 years. Measured on synthetic values (`TestNinetyDaysOfAGlobalGridStayReadable`, and the same generator for a day of hourly records): a 2° MUF grid's roll-ups take about 5.5 MB on disk a month (about 330 MB at 5 years), and its hourly records 1,454,432 bytes a day. (The agent put the hourly figure to the HUM LEAD as "measured" before measuring it; it was measured afterwards, and the estimate held.) 0.18.0's datasets are far smaller (NDFD about 14.5 KB a day of roll-up). A bound removes the oldest files first, roll-up parts and then days, which is earlier than the retention the user chose. Options: per dataset, said in the Data tab; only where declared; one store-wide bound | "Per dataset, said (Rec.)" | **Every dataset declares its own byte bound, sized for it.** 0.18.0's bounds sit above anything the longest retention holds at their measured rates, so nothing they keep changes. MUF's bound is set at W4 with its dataset. Past its bound, a dataset's oldest files go first: roll-ups, then days, never the current day. **When a bound cuts a dataset short of its retention, the Data tab says so.** |

| D-186 | 2026-10-14 | go-ionomaps G6.2: R-2.4 asks for "the published method behind ITU-R P.533's absorption term ... never copied text or tables", but P.533-14 eq. (20) reads its three factors (ATnoon, the penetration factor phi, the diurnal exponent p) only from Figs. 1 to 3, with no published fit; the only machine-readable forms are fits and a table inside ITU-R Study Group 3's ITUHFProp (and NTIA's REC533, its source), from unpublished CCIR working documents (desk survey, 2026-10-14, kept in the build log's evidence; ITUHFProp's README: "may be used by implementers in their implementation of the Recommendation ... free from any copyright assertions", no LICENSE file; NTIA's VOACAP terms: "use, copy, modify, and redistribute ... by keeping this exact text present"). The survey read ITUHFProp's and VOACAP's source to describe them; nothing was copied into either tree; ITU's repository's latest commit merges a Makefile change by the GitHub user `arodland`, a different repository from `arodland/prop`, which was not opened. Options: ITUHFProp's fits; VOACAP's foE-driven absorption (CCIR 252-2); digitise P.533's figures; defer absorption. Counter-argument: the first copies tables, which R-2.4 forbids, on a README statement, not a licence file | "ITUHFProp's fits (Rec.)" | **Regular daytime absorption is P.533-14 eq. (20), its ATnoon table and its phi and p fits ported from ITUHFProp**, credited in go-ionomaps' NOTICE with ITU-R SG3's statement; ITUHFProp, built locally outside the tree, is the test oracle for the absorption loss; **R-2.4 is amended to allow this one source**, every other table still never copied. |
| D-187 | 2026-10-14 | go-ionomaps G6.2: which noise floor decides "absorbed" for the reference circuit (100 W SSB, +13 dB in 2.5 kHz, D-91)? ITU-R P.372-18 gives man-made noise as a formula a category, Fam = c - d log f (Table 1: City 76.8/27.7, Residential 72.5/27.7, Rural 67.2/27.7, Quiet rural 53.6/28.6; at 3 MHz -140, -145, -150, -164 dBW/Hz), and galactic noise as 52 - 23 log f, heard above foF2 only; atmospheric noise needs ITU's noise-map data, outside R-2.4, and VOACAP's guide (Lane, 2001, p. 3-4) finds man-made noise the controlling source by day. Options: the host's choice per update, Residential by default; fixed Residential; fixed Rural. Counter-argument: one more per-update option for watchpost to expose, and the answer moves with it | "Host's choice, Residential (Rec.)" | **`UpdateOptions` gains the P.372 environment - City, Residential, Rural, Quiet rural - Residential by default**; the noise is that category's man-made noise plus galactic noise above foF2; a change needs no request, as the no-readings correction (P-1); **atmospheric noise is not modelled, and the library says so**. |
| D-188 | 2026-10-14 | go-ionomaps G6.2: what gain have the reference circuit's "simple antennas" (D-73) at each end? P.533-14 adds the transmitting gain to the field strength (eq. 17) and the receiving gain to the available power (eq. 43: Prw = Ew + Grw - 20 log f - 107.2 dBW), so each dB at either end moves the "absorbed" line by a dB. Options: 0 dBi both ends; 2.15 dBi (a dipole in free space); the host's choice, 0 dBi by default. Counter-argument: a real dipole does better than 0 dBi in its best direction, so isotropic calls some bands absorbed a ham with a dipole would work | "0 dBi, both ends (Rec.)" | **The reference circuit's antennas are isotropic, 0 dBi at both ends**: no pattern, height or direction; one figure for every host and operator. |

(33) (§5.3.2) - which D-186's exception does not cover. Extend it to both; omit both (Lh = 0, Aw = 1), stated; or take Table 2 only and use §5.2's penetration points at every length? Counter-argument: it widens a no-copy exception granted the same day, and both are ITU "all rights reserved" tables carried in ITUHFProp under the same README statement | "Extend to both (Rec.)" | **D-186's exception extends to P.533-14 Tables 2 and 5, taken from ITUHFProp**, credited in NOTICE: Lh at control points above 42.5 degrees geomagnetic latitude, and §5.3's long-path LUF with its winter anomaly beyond 7,000 km, interpolated from 7,000 to 9,000 km by eq. (42). Every other table is still never copied. |

(39), needs fM; D-189 opened Tables 2 and 5 only. Extend to Table 3 and build §5.3; stop the lower limit at 7000 km, said; or use §5.2 at every length. | D-195 | 2026-10-15 | UAT (batch 23, `31cbbead`): "'g' does not open the Map Modal in Broadcaster UI mode when no other modal is active" and "'g' DOES open the map modal in broadcaster UI mode when another modal is opened first". Found: the console's key map binds no map action (`modes/tty/router.go:159`), so `g` reaches the console and does nothing; with a window open every key goes to Observer (`modes/tty/router.go:656`), whose `g` opens its own map, in a weather mode, stacked over that window. FR-2.1 (W7, D-57) was unbuilt. The HUM LEAD: "For Broadcaster, we want the map to be UI MODE specific: 1. 'g' Opens the map, centered on 'my location' (either city, zip, or GPS as the user inputed) 2. a red braile circle that matches the configured service radius should be drawn and always present (because I care about the area I'm currently servicing) 3. Area inside the service circle should have a tint using our computational methods similar to alert overlays 4. 1 - 2 are always shown in broadcaster mode regardless of overlay mode and zoom level (circle grows/shrinks depending on zoom level) 5. | D-197 | 2026-10-15 | D-193's promised follow-up: should the answers name a best frequency? ITU-R P.373 defines the FOT as the frequency the operational MUF exceeds on 90 % of days; for F2 paths it is commonly taken as about 85 % of the MUF. The MUF here is the hour's estimate, not a monthly median, so the factor is a rule of thumb and must be said as one. (3) a store that only trimmed the current version would empty it while an old version held the bytes.
